Given uncertainty, the safe design: descend until contact is detectable, then release, then retreat up.

Practical robust plan: descend in 2 cm steps to a conservative height, then in 1cm steps, checking the agentview for the bowl bottom touching the cabinet top, and monitor `final_dist_m` stalls. Since the bowl hangs with its rim ~1cm below the eef? Let's estimate: rim ≈ 1.24 (bowl top), eef = 1.2494 → rim ≈ eef - 0.01. Bowl bottom ≈ rim - 0.045 ≈ eef - 0.055.

So target: bowl bottom at 1.127 → eef z ≈ 1.182. Add a small margin: descend to eef z = 1.19 (bowl bottom ~1.135, 8mm above the top), then release; bowl falls 8mm onto the top. That's a gentle drop.

Hmm, but wait: bowl height. If the bowl is 0.06 tall then bottom = eef - 0.07 → eef 1.20.

Since the drop is small either way, releasing at eef z=1.195 will drop ≤ 1.5cm. Good, and no crushing risk (which is important: pressing too hard could slip the marginal grip).

But there's a subtlety: when the bowl is released while the fingers are pinching its rim, the fingers open and the bowl drops — it might tip if the pinch is off-center (it's pinched on one side of the rim, so on release the bowl may rotate). A ~1.5 cm drop of a bowl should settle flat.

Alternative safer: descend to contact (bowl bottom on the top) then release with the gripper gently opening, and retreat straight up. Contact means the bowl's weight is supported by the top surface — then releasing can't tip it, and no fall at all. That's better if I know the offset. Since the bowl bottom is ~eef-0.055 (uncertain ±7mm), descending to eef z = 1.175 would either touch (good) or press ~7mm (slight squash) — pressing a bowl against a rigid top with a marginal pinch grip risks it popping out. Hmm.

Compromise: descend slowly to eef z = 1.19, check the agentview to see if the bowl is on/near the top, then do 1cm steps while watching; stop when the bowl visibly rests (z stops decreasing). Actually when the bowl touches, further descent makes the fingers stay while the bowl is blocked → the fingers would slide down around the bowl. So I can detect a stall (final_dist_m > tol).

```
def _eval_predicate_on(self, obj, support, ...):
    if not (self._check_obj_in_region...)
```
Actually LIBERO uses:
```
def on(self, object1, object2):
    # object1 is on object2
    ...
```
Hmm. In libero (libero/lbddl or libero table env), the On predicate check: `get_position(object1)` bottom z >= support top z - tol, and horizontal: `check_contact`? I recall many agents succeed by placing the object "on top" with generous margins since the check is typically:
```
support_pos = support's top-surface bbox expanded
return obj_xy in support_bbox and obj_bottom_z >= support_top_z - 0.01
```
There is likely a shrink factor of 0.5 or so. Being well-centered is safest.
